And if any bishop from this time forward is found consecrating a temple without holy relics, he shall be deposed, as a transgressor of the ecclesiastical traditions.
¶136
Παῦλος ὁ θεῖος Ἀπόστολός φησι· Τινῶν ἀνθρώπων αἱ ἁμαρτίαι πρόδηλοί εἰσι, τισὶ δὲ καὶ ἐπακολουθοῦσι. Πρόδηλοι μὲν αὗται, αἱ ἕτεραι δὲ τούτων μετ᾽ αὐτὰς εἰσίν. Ἐπὶ γοῦν τῇ αἱρέσει τῶν συκοφαντῶν τῶν Χριστιανῶν, ἄλλη δυσσέβεια παρακολουθοῦσα εὕρηται. Ὥσπερ γὰρ τὴν καθέδραν τῶν σεπτῶν εἰκόνων ἐκ τῶν ἐκκλησιῶν ἐξήλασαν, οὕτω δὴ καὶ τὰ λοιπὰ ἔθη ῥίψαντες, νῦν ἀνανεοῦσθαι ταῦτα κατὰ νόμον ἔγγραφόν τε καὶ ἄγραφον σπουδάζομεν. Θεσπίζομεν τοίνυν, εἴ τινες σεβάσμιοι ναοὶ χωρὶς καταθέσεως λειψάνων μαρτύρων καθιερώθησαν, ἐν τούτοις κατάθεσιν λειψάνων γίνεσθαι, μετὰ τῆς συνήθους εὐχῆς. Εἰ δέ τις εὑρεθείη τῶν ἐπισκόπων ἀπὸ τοῦ νῦν ναὸν καθιερῶν χωρὶς ἁγίων λειψάνων, καθαιρείσθω, ὡς παραβάτης τῶν ἐκκλησιαστικῶν παραδόσεων.
Ancient Epitome: Let relics of the Holy Martyrs be placed in such churches as have been consecrated without them, and this with the accustomed prayers. But whoever shall consecrate a church without these shall be deposed as a transgressor of the traditions of the Church.
¶137
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Ναοὶ ὅσοι χωρὶς λειψάνων μαρτύρων καθιερώθησαν, ἐν τούτοις γίνεσθαι κατάθεσιν λειψάνων, μετὰ τῆς συνήθους εὐχῆς. Ὁ δὲ χωρὶς τούτων καθιερῶν, καθαιρείσθω, ὡς παραβάτης τῶν ἐκκλησιαστικῶν παραδόσεων.
That Hebrews ought not to be received unless they have been converted in sincerity of heart.
¶138
ΚΑΝΩΝ Η΄
CANON VIII
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν Ἑβραίους δέχεσθαι, εἰ μὴ προηγουμένως καθαρῶς ἐπιστραφεῖεν.
Since certain, erring in the superstitions of the Hebrews, have thought to mock at Christ our God, and feigning to be converted to the religion of Christ do deny him, and in private and secretly keep the Sabbath and observe other Jewish customs, we decree that such persons be not received to communion, nor to prayers, nor into the Church; but let them be openly Hebrews according to their religion, and let them not bring their children to baptism, nor purchase or possess a slave. But if any of them, out of a sincere heart and in faith, is converted and makes profession with his whole heart, setting at naught their customs and observances, and so that others may be convinced and converted, such an one is to be received and baptized, and his children likewise; and let them be taught to take care to hold aloof from the ordinances of the Hebrews. But if they will not do this, let them in no way be received.
¶139
Ἐπειδή τινες τῇ τῶν Ἑβραίων θρησκείᾳ πλανώμενοι, ἐφρόνησαν Χριστὸν τὸν Θεὸν ἡμῶν χλευάσαι, προσποιούμενοι Χριστιανοὺς γίνεσθαι, ἀρνοῦνται δὲ αὐτὸν κρυφῇ, καὶ κρυπτῶς σαββατίζοντες, καὶ ἕτερα Ἰουδαϊκὰ ἐπιτελοῦντες, ὁρίζομεν τούτους μήτε κοινωνίας, μήτε εὐχῆς, μήτε εἰς ἐκκλησίαν δέχεσθαι· ἀλλὰ εἶναι φανερῶς Ἑβραίους κατὰ τὴν αὐτῶν θρησκείαν, μήτε τὰ ἑαυτῶν τέκνα βαπτίζειν, μήτε δοῦλον κτᾶσθαι, ἢ κεκτῆσθαι. Εἰ δέ τις ἐξ εἰλικρινοῦς καρδίας καὶ πίστεως ἐπιστραφείη, καὶ ἐξ ὅλης καρδίας ὁμολογήσειε, κατὰ τοὺς αὐτῶν τρόπους σαββατίζων, καὶ τὰ λοιπὰ Ἰουδαϊκὰ μηδὲν ποιῶν· τούτους χρὴ δέχεσθαι, καὶ βαπτίζειν αὐτούς τε καὶ τὰ τέκνα αὐτῶν, καὶ οὕτω φυλάττειν, ὡς καὶ πόρρωθεν Ἱουδαϊκῶν ἐθισμῶν εἶναι. Εἰ δὲ μὴ τοῦτο ποιοῖεν, μηδ᾽ ὅλως αὐτοὺς δέχεσθαι.
That none of the books containing the heresy of the traducers of the Christians are to be hid.
¶140
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Ἑβραίους μὴ δέχεσθαι, εἰ μὴ φανερῶς καθαρῶς ἐπιστραφεῖεν.
Ancient Epitome: Hebrews must not be received unless they are manifestly converted with sincerity of heart.
ΚΑΝΩΝ Θ΄
CANON IX
Περὶ τοῦ μηδὲν βιβλίον φυλάσσειν περιέχον τὴν αἵρεσιν τῶν συκοφαντῶν τῶν Χριστιανῶν.
All the childish devices and mad ravings which have been falsely written against the venerable images, must be delivered up to the Episcopium of Constantinople, that they may be locked away with other heretical books. And if anyone is found hiding such books, if he be a bishop or presbyter or deacon, let him be deposed; but if he be a monk or layman, let him be anathema.
¶141
Πάσας τὰς παιγνιώδεις ληρώδεις τε καὶ μανιώδεις ψευδογραφίας, τὰς κατὰ τῶν σεπτῶν εἰκόνων γενομένας, τῷ ἐπισκοπείῳ Κωνσταντινουπόλεως ἀποτίθεσθαι, μετὰ τῶν λοιπῶν αἱρετικῶν βιβλίων. Εἰ δέ τις εὑρεθείη κρύπτων τὰ τοιαῦτα, εἰ μὲν ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ πρεσβύτερος, ἢ διάκονος, καθαιρείσθω· εἰ δὲ μοναχὸς, ἢ λαϊκὸς, ἀναθεματιζέσθω.
Ancient Epitome: If any one is found to have concealed a book written against the venerable images, if he is on the clergy list let him be deposed; if a layman or monk let him be cut off.
¶142
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Εἴ τις εὑρεθείη βιβλίον κρύπτων κατὰ τῶν σεπτῶν εἰκόνων γραφέν, εἰ μὲν κληρικὸς, καθαιρείσθω· εἰ δὲ λαϊκὸς ἢ μοναχὸς, ἀναθεματιζέσθω.
That no cleric ought to leave his diocese and go into another without the knowledge of the Bishop.
¶143
ΚΑΝΩΝ Ι΄
CANON X
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν κληρικὸν ἐκ τῆς ἰδίας παροικίας εἰς ἑτέραν ἀνεννοήτως βαδίζειν.
Since certain of the clergy, misinterpreting the canonical constitutions, leave their own diocese and run into other dioceses, especially into this God-protected royal city, and take up their abode with princes, celebrating liturgies in their oratories, it is not permitted to receive such persons into any house or church without the license of their own Bishop and also that of the Bishop of Constantinople. And if any clerk shall do this without such license, and shall so continue, let him be deposed. With regard to those who have done this with the knowledge of the aforesaid Bishops, it is not lawful for them to undertake mundane and secular responsibilities, since this is forbidden by the sacred canons. And if anyone is discovered holding the office of those who are called Meizoteroi; let him either lay it down, or be deposed from the priesthood. Let him rather be the instructor of the children and others of the household, reading to them the Divine Scriptures, for to this end he received the priesthood.
¶144
Ἐπειδή τινες τῶν κληρικῶν, τοὺς κανονικοὺς διαστρεψάμενοι θεσμοὺς, καταλείπουσι τὴν ἰδίαν παροικίαν, εἰς ἑτέρας δὲ παρατρέχουσι παροικίας, μάλιστα τὴν θεοφύλακτον ταύτην βασιλίδα πόλιν, καὶ καταλαγχάνουσί τισιν ἄρχουσι, λειτουργοῦντες ἐν τοῖς εὐκτηρίοις αὐτῶν, οὐ συγκεχώρηται δέχεσθαι τοὺς τοιούτους, μήτε ἐν οἴκῳ, μήτε ἐν ἐκκλησίᾳ, χωρὶς γνώμης τοῦ τε ἰδίου ἐπισκόπου, καὶ τοῦ Κωνσταντινουπόλεως. Εἰ δέ τις κληρικὸς τοῦτο πεποιήσει χωρὶς γνώμης τῆς προειρημένης, καὶ οὕτω διαμένοι, καθαιρείσθω. Τοὺς δὲ μετὰ γνώμης τῶν προειρημένων ἐπισκόπων τοῦτο πεποιηκότας, οὐκ ἔξεστι κοσμικὰς καὶ σαρκικὰς ἀναδέχεσθαι φροντίδας· ἀπείρηται γὰρ τοῦτο τοῖς ἱεροῖς κανόσι. Εἰ δέ τις εὑρεθείη τὰς τῶν λεγομένων μειζοτέρων πληρῶν διακονίας, ἤτοι τοῦτο καταλιμπανέτω, ἢ καθαιρείσθω. Παιδευέτω δὲ μᾶλλον τοὺς παῖδας, καὶ τοὺς λοιποὺς τοῦ οἴκου, ἀναγινώσκων αὐτοῖς τὰ θεῖα λόγια· εἰς τοῦτο γὰρ τὴν ἱερωσύνην ἐδέξατο.
Ancient Epitome: A clergyman who after leaving his own parish has settled in another far off from his own bishop and from the bishop of Constantinople, shall be received neither into house nor church. And if he shall persevere in his course, he shall be deposed. But if they shall do this with a knowledge of what we have said, they shall not receive a secular position; or should they have received them, they shall cease from them. And if they refuse they shall be deposed.
¶145
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Κληρικὸς μετὰ τὸ ἀπολιπεῖν τὴν ἰδίαν παροικίαν, κατοικίσας ἑαυτὸν ἐν ἑτέρᾳ μακρὰν τοῦ ἰδίου ἐπισκόπου, καὶ τοῦ Κωνσταντινουπόλεως, μήτε εἰς οἶκον, μήτε εἰς ἐκκλησίαν δεχέσθω. Εἰ δὲ ἐπιμένοι, καθαιρείσθω. Εἰ δὲ μετὰ γνώμης τῶν εἰρημένων ταῦτα πράξειαν, κοσμικὰς ἀρχὰς μὴ περιποιείσθωσαν· εἰ δὲ κέκτηνται, παυέσθωσαν· εἰ δὲ μή, καθαιρείσθωσαν.
That Œconomi ought to be in the Episcopal palaces and in the Monasteries.
¶146
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΑ΄
CANON XI
Περὶ τοῦ δεῖν οἰκονόμους εἶναι ἐν τοῖς ἐπισκοπείοις καὶ ἐν τοῖς μοναστηρίοις.
Since we are under obligation to guard all the divine canons, we ought by all means to maintain in its integrity that one which says œconomi are to be in each church. If the metropolitan appoints in his Church an œconomus, he does well; but if he does not, it is permitted to the Bishop of Constantinople by his own authority to choose an œconomus for the Church of the Metropolitan. A like authority belongs to the metropolitans, if the Bishops who are subject to them do not wish to appoint œconomi in their churches. The same rule is also to be observed with respect to monasteries.
¶147
Ἀνάγκην ἔχομεν φυλάττειν πάντας τοὺς θείους κανόνας· ὅθεν καὶ τὸν λέγοντα οἰκονόμους ἐν ἑκάστῃ ἐκκλησίᾳ εἶναι, πάσῃ δυνάμει κρατύνομεν. Εἰ μὲν οὖν ὁ μητροπολίτης ἐν τῇ ἑαυτοῦ ἐκκλησίᾳ προβάλλοιτο οἰκονόμον, καλῶς ποιεῖ· εἰ δὲ μή, τῷ Κωνσταντινουπόλεως ἐπισκόπῳ δίδοται ἐξουσία, ἰδίᾳ αὐθεντίᾳ, ἐκλέξασθαι οἰκονόμον τῇ τοῦ μητροπολίτου ἐκκλησίᾳ. Τὸ αὐτὸ δίδοται καὶ τοῖς μητροπολίταις, εἴγε οἱ ὑπ᾽ αὐτοὺς ἐπίσκοποι μὴ βούλοιντο προβάλλεσθαι οἰκονόμους ταῖς ἑαυτῶν ἐκκλησίαις. Τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ περὶ μοναστηρίων φυλάττεσθαι.
Ancient Epitome: If the Metropolitan does not elect an œconomus of the metropolis, the patriarch shall do so. If the bishop shall not do so, the Metropolitan shall; for so it seemed good to the fathers assembled at Chalcedon. The same law shall hold in monasteries.
¶148
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Εἰ μὲν μητροπολίτης μὴ χειροτονήσει οἰκονόμον μητροπόλεως, ὁ πατριάρχης· εἰ δὲ μὴ ἐπίσκοπος, ὁ μητροπολίτης· οὕτω γὰρ τοῖς ἐν Χαλκηδόνι πατράσιν ἔδοξε. Μονάζουσι δὲ ὁ αὐτὸς νόμος.
That a Bishop or Hegumenos ought not to alienate any part of the suburban estate of the church.
¶149
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΒ΄
CANON XII
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν ἐπίσκοπον ἢ ἡγούμενον προδοῦναί τινα τῶν ὑπαγρων τῆς ἐκκλησίας.
If bishop or hegumenos is found alienating any part of the farm lands of the bishoprick or monastery into the hands of secular princes, or surrendering them to any other person, such act is null according to the canon of the holy Apostles, which says: Let the bishop take care of all the Church's goods, and let him administer the same according as in the sight of God. It is not lawful for him to appropriate any part himself, or to confer upon his relations the things which belong to God. If they are poor let them be helped among the poor; but let them not be used as a pretext for smuggling away the Church's property. And if it be urged that the land is only a loss and yields no profit, the place is not on that account to be given to the secular rulers, who are in the neighbourhood; but let it be given to clergymen or husbandmen. And if they have resorted to dishonest craft, so that the ruler has bought the land from the husbandman or cleric, such transaction shall likewise be null, and the land shall be restored to the bishoprick or monastery. And the bishop or hegumenos doing this shall be turned out, the bishop from his bishoprick and the hegumenos from his monastery, as those who wasted what they did not gather.
¶150
Εἴ τις ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ ἡγούμενος εὑρεθείη, τῶν τῆς ἐπισκοπῆς ἢ μοναστηρίου χωραφίων τι, προσκυροῦντα εἰς χεῖρας ἀρχόντων, ἢ προσωπικῶς ἑτέρῳ παραχωροῦντα, κατὰ τὸν τῶν ἁγίων Ἀποστόλων κανόνα, τὸν λέγοντα· Προσεχέτω ὁ ἐπίσκοπος πᾶσι τοῖς ἐκκλησιαστικοῖς, καὶ διοικείτω αὐτὰ ὡς Θεοῦ ἐφορῶντος. Μὴ ἐξεῖναι αὐτῷ τῶν οἰκείων τι ἰδιώσασθαι, ἢ τοῖς ἑαυτοῦ συγγενέσι, τὰ τοῦ Θεοῦ χαρίζεσθαι. Εἰ δὲ πένητες τυγχάνουσι, δίδοσθαι αὐτοῖς ὡς πένησι, μὴ προφάσει τούτου τὰ τῆς ἐκκλησίας διαπωλεῖσθαι. Καὶ εἰ λυσιτελῆ λέγοιτο μηδὲν φέρειν, μὴ ἀπὸ τούτου τοῖς γειτνιῶσιν ἄρχουσι τὸν τόπον δίδοσθαι, ἀλλὰ κληρικοῖς, ἢ γεωργοῖς. Εἰ δὲ δόλῳ πονηρῷ κεχρημένοι, πρίαιτο παρὰ γεωργοῦ, ἢ κληρικοῦ, ὁ ἄρχων τὸν τόπον, ἄκυρον κἀκεῖνο, καὶ ἀποκαθίστασθαι τῇ ἐπισκοπῇ, ἢ τῷ μοναστηρίῳ τὸν τόπον. Καὶ ὁ τοῦτο ποιῶν ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ ἡγούμενος, ἐκβαλλέσθω, ἐπίσκοπος μὲν τῆς ἐπισκοπῆς, ἡγούμενος δὲ τοῦ μοναστηρίου· ὡς τὰ μὴ συναχθέντα σκορπίσαντες.
Ancient Epitome: According to what seemed good to the Holy Apostles, any act of alienation of the goods of a diocese or of a monastery made by the bishop, or by the superior of the monastery, shall be null. And the Bishop or Superior who shall have done this shall be expelled.
¶151
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Τὰ παραχωρούμενα παρὰ τοῦ ἐπισκόπου, ἢ τοῦ ἡγουμένου, τοῖς τῆς ἐπισκοπῆς ἢ μοναστηρίου κτήμασιν, ἄκυρα, κατὰ τὸ δόξαν τοῖς ἁγίοις Ἀποστόλοις· καὶ ὁ τοῦτο πεποιηκὼς ἐκβαλλέσθω.
That they are worthy of special condemnation who turn the monasteries into public houses.
¶152
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΓ΄
CANON XIII
Περὶ τοῦ ἐνόχους τυγχάνειν τοὺς ἀποστρέφοντας τὰ μοναστήρια εἰς καταγώγια.
During the calamity which was brought to pass in the Churches, because of our sins, some of the sacred houses, for example, bishops' palaces and monasteries, were seized by certain men and became public inns. If those who now hold them choose to give them back, so that they may be restored to their original use, well and good; but if not, and these persons are on the sacerdotal list, we command that they be deposed; if they be monks or laymen, that they be excommunicated, as those who have been condemned from the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Ghost, and assigned their place where the worm dies not and the fire is not quenched, because they set themselves against the voice of the Lord, which says: Make not my Father's house a house of merchandise.
¶153
Ἐπὶ τῆς γενομένης συμφορᾶς ταῖς ἐκκλησίαις διὰ τὰς ἁμαρτίας ἡμῶν, ἁρπαγέντες τινὲς τῶν ἱερῶν οἴκων, ἤτοι ἐπισκοπεῖα, ἢ μοναστήρια, παρὰ τινων, γεγόνασι καταγώγια. Εἰ μὲν οὖν βούλονται ταῦτα νῦν κατέχοντες, ἀποδοῦναι, ἵνα εἰς τὸ ἀρχαῖον ἀποκατασταθῇ σχῆμα, καλῶς ἔχει· εἰ δὲ μή, καὶ εἶεν εἰς ἱερατικὸν κατάλογον τελοῦντες, καθαιρεῖσθαι· εἰ δὲ μοναχοὶ, ἢ λαϊκοί, ἀκοινωνήτους εἶναι, ὡς κατακεκριμένους ἀπὸ Πατρὸς, καὶ Υἱοῦ, καὶ ἁγίου Πνεύματος, καὶ ἐν τῷ τόπῳ τεταγμένους, οὗ ὁ σκώληξ οὐ τελευτᾷ, καὶ τὸ πῦρ οὐ σβέννυται, ὅτι τῇ Κυριακῇ ἐναντιοῦνται φωνῇ, λεγούσῃ· Μὴ ποιεῖτε τὸν οἶκον τοῦ Πατρός μου οἶκον ἐμπορίου.
Ancient Epitome: Those who make common diocesan or monastic goods, unless they restore to the bishop or superior the things belonging to the diocese or monastery, the whole proceeding shall be null. If they are persons in Holy Orders they shall be deposed, but if laymen or monks they shall be cast out.
¶154
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Οἱ κοινὰ ποιοῦντες ἐπισκοπικά, ἢ μοναστηριακά, εἰ μὴ ἀποκαταστήσουσι τῷ ἐπισκόπῳ, ἢ τῷ ἡγουμένῳ τὰ τῆς ἐπισκοπῆς, ἢ μοναστηρίου, ἄκυρον πᾶν. Εἰ μὲν εἰσιν ἱερατικοὶ, καθαιρεῖσθωσαν· εἰ δὲ μοναχοὶ, ἢ λαϊκοὶ, ἀκοινώνητοι.
That no one without ordination ought to read in the ambo during the synaxis.
¶155
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΔ΄
CANON XIV
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν τινὰ χωρὶς χειροθεσίας ἀναγινώσκειν ἐν τῷ ἄμβωνι κατὰ τὴν σύναξιν.
That there is a certain order established in the priesthood is very evident to all, and to guard diligently the promotions of the priesthood is well pleasing to God. Since therefore we see certain youths who have received the clerical tonsure, but who have not yet received ordination from the bishop, reading in the ambo during the Synaxis, and in doing this violating the canons, we forbid this to be done (from henceforth,) and let this prohibition be observed also among the monks. It is permitted to each hegumenos in his own monastery to ordain a reader, if he himself had received the laying on of hands by a bishop to the dignity of hegumenos, and is known to be a presbyter. Chorepiscopi may likewise, according to ancient custom and with the bishop's authorization, appoint readers.
¶156
Ὅτι τάξις ἐστὶν ἐν τοῖς ἱερεῦσι, πᾶσι δῆλον, καὶ τὸ φυλάττειν ἀκριβῶς τὰ προβιβάσματα τῆς ἱερωσύνης τῷ Θεῷ εὐάρεστον. Ἐπεὶ οὖν ὁρῶμέν τινας παῖδας κουρὰν μὲν κληρικὴν δεξαμένους, χειροθεσίαν δὲ παρὰ ἐπισκόπου μήπω εἰληφότας, ἐν τῷ ἄμβωνι ἀναγινώσκοντας κατὰ τὴν σύναξιν, καὶ τοῦτο ποιοῦντας κανόνος ἐκδιαίτημα, κωλύομεν τοῦ λοιποῦ τοῦτο γίνεσθαι. Τηρείσθω δὲ τὸ αὐτὸ κἀπὶ τῶν μοναχῶν. Συγκεχώρηται δὲ ἑκάστῳ ἡγουμένῳ ἐν τῷ ἑαυτοῦ μοναστηρίῳ χειροτονεῖν ἀναγνώστην, εἴ γε τὴν χειροθεσίαν αὐτὸς παρὰ ἐπισκόπου εἰς ἡγουμένου τάξιν ἐδέξατο, πρεσβύτερος ὑπάρχων. Ὡσαύτως δὲ καὶ τοὺς χωρεπισκόπους, κατὰ ἀρχαῖον ἔθος, μετὰ γνώμης τοῦ ἐπισκόπου, ἀναγνώστας προβάλλεσθαι.
Ancient Epitome: No one shall read from the ambon unless he has been ordained by the bishop. And this shall be in force also among monks. The superior of a monastery, if he has been ordained by the bishop, may ordain a lector but only in his own monastery. A chorepiscopus also can make a lector.
¶157
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Μὴ ἀναγινώσκειν ἀπὸ ἄμβωνος τὸν μὴ χειροτονηθέντα παρὰ ἐπισκόπου. Τοῦτο κρατείτω κἀπὶ μοναχῶν. Ἡγούμενος μοναστηρίου, εἰ μετὰ χειροθεσίαν γέγονεν ἡγούμενος παρὰ ἐπισκόπου, χειροτονήσει ἀναγνώστην τῷ ἰδίῳ μοναστηρίῳ. Καὶ χωρεπίσκοπος ποιεῖ ἀναγνώστην.
That a clerk ought not to be set over two churches.
¶158
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΕ΄
CANON XV
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν ἐν δύο ἐκκλησίαις κληρικὸν ἐγκαθίστασθαι.
From henceforth no clergyman shall be appointed over two churches; for this savours of merchandise and filthy lucre, and is altogether alien from ecclesiastical custom. We have heard by the very voice of the Lord that, No man can serve two masters, for either he will hate the one and love the other, or else he will hold to the one and despise the other. Each one, therefore, as says the Apostle, in the calling wherein he was called, in the same he ought to abide, and in one only church to give attendance. For in the affairs of the Church, what is gained through filthy lucre is altogether separate from God. To meet the necessities of this life, there are various occupations, by means of which, if one so desire, let him procure the things needful for the body. For says the Apostle, These hands have ministered unto my necessities, and to them that were with me. Occupations of this sort may be obtained in the God-protected city. But in the country places outside, because of the small number of people, let a dispensation be granted.
¶159
Ἀπὸ τοῦ νῦν μηδένα κληρικὸν ἐπὶ δύο ἐκκλησίας καθίστασθαι· τοῦτο γὰρ ἐμπορίας καὶ αἰσχροκερδείας ὄζει, καὶ παντελῶς ἐκκλησιαστικοῦ ἔθους ἀλλότριόν ἐστι. Ἀκηκόαμεν γὰρ αὐτῆς τῆς Κυριακῆς φωνῆς, ὅτι Οὐδεὶς δύναται δυσὶ κυρίοις δουλεύειν· ἢ γὰρ τὸν ἕνα μισήσει, καὶ τὸν ἕτερον ἀγαπήσει· ἢ ἑνὸς ἀνθέξεται, καὶ τοῦ ἑτέρου καταφρονήσει. Ἕκαστος οὖν, κατὰ τὴν Ἀποστολικὴν φωνήν, ἐν τῇ κλήσει ᾗ ἐκλήθη, ἐν ταύτῃ μενέτω, καὶ ἐν μιᾷ εὑρίσκεσθω ἐκκλησίᾳ. Ἐν γὰρ τοῖς τῆς ἐκκλησίας πράγμασι, τὸ αἰσχροκερδῶς κτώμενον, παντελῶς τοῦ Θεοῦ ξένον τυγχάνει. Πρὸς δὲ τὰς τοῦ βίου χρείας, διάφοροί εἰσιν ἐργασίαι, δι᾽ ὧν, εἴ τις βούλοιτο, τὰ ἐπιτήδεια τῷ σώματι περιποιείσθω. Φησὶ γὰρ ὁ Ἀπόστολος· Αὗται αἱ χεῖρες πρὸς τὰς χρείας μου, καὶ τῶν ὄντων μετ᾽ ἐμοῦ ὑπηρέτησαν. Καὶ ταύτας μὲν ἐνταῦθα τῇ θεοφυλάκτῳ πόλει κτᾶσθαι· ἐν δὲ τοῖς ἔξω χωρίοις, διὰ τὴν σπάνιν τῶν ἀνθρώπων, συγχώρησιν γίνεσθαι.
Ancient Epitome: Hereafter at Constantinople a cleric may not serve two churches. But in the outskirts this may be permitted on account of the scarcity of men.
¶160
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Κληρικὸς ἐν Κωνσταντινουπόλει δύο ἐκκλησίαις μὴ λειτουργείτω· ἐν δὲ τοῖς ἔξω διὰ σπάνιν ἀνθρώπων συγχωρείσθω.
That it does not become one in holy orders to be clad in costly apparel.
¶161
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙϚ΄
CANON XVI
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν τὸν ἱερωμένον λαμπρᾷ περιβολῇ κεχρῆσθαι.
All buffoonery and decking of the body ill becomes the priestly rank. Therefore those bishops and clerics who array themselves in gay and showy clothing ought to correct themselves, and if they do not amend they ought to be subjected to punishment. So likewise they who anoint themselves with perfumes. When the root of bitterness sprang up, there was poured into the Catholic Church the pollution of the heresy of the traducers of the Christians. And such as were defiled by it, not only detested the pictured images, but also set at naught all decorum, being exceedingly mad against those who lived gravely and religiously; so that in them was fulfilled that which is written, The service of God is abominable to the sinner. If therefore, any are found deriding those who are clad in poor and grave raiment, let them be corrected by punishment. For from early times every man in holy orders wore modest and grave clothing; and verily whatever is worn, not so much because of necessity, but for the sake of outward show, savours of dandyism, as says Basil the Great. Nor did anyone array himself in raiment embroidered with silk, nor put many-coloured ornaments on the border of his garments; for they had heard from the lips of God that They that wear soft clothing are in kings' houses.
¶162
Πᾶσα γελοιότης, καὶ περιβολὴ σώματος, ἱερατικῷ σχήματι ἀλλότριον. Οὕτω τοὺς ἐπισκόπους, ἢ κληρικοὺς, κοσμικῇ καὶ ἐπιδείξει σχημάτων στολιζομένους, χρὴ ἑαυτοὺς ἐπανορθοῦν· εἰ δὲ ἐπιμένοιεν, διορθοῦσθαι. Τοὺς δὲ μύροις χριομένους, ὁμοίως. Ἀπὸ γὰρ τῆς πικρίας ταύτης, φυέντος τοῦ ζιζανίου, ἡ τῆς αἱρέσεως τῶν συκοφαντῶν τῶν Χριστιανῶν κηκὶς τῇ καθολικῇ ἐκκλησίᾳ συνεχύθη. Οἱ γὰρ ἐκείνης μολυνθέντες, οὐ μόνον εἰκόνων μισήμασι προσέσχον, ἀλλὰ καὶ πάντων εὐσχημοσύνης κατεφρόνησαν, ἄγαν μαινόμενοι κατὰ τῶν σεμνοτέρων τε καὶ εὐλαβῶς πολιτευομένων, ὡς πληρωθῆναι ἐν αὐτοῖς τὸ γεγραμμένον· Τῷ ἁμαρτωλῷ δυσχερὲς ἡ εὐσέβεια. Εἴ τινες οὖν εὑρεθεῖεν χλευάζοντες τοὺς ἐν εὐτελεῖ καὶ σεμνῇ περιβολῇ ἀναστρεφομένους, διορθούσθωσαν ζημίᾳ. Ἐκ παλαιοῦ γὰρ πᾶς ὁ ἱερωμένος μέτριοις καὶ σεμνοῖς περιβλήμασι κέχρηται. Πᾶν γὰρ ὅπερ ἂν φορῇ μὴ πρὸς ἀνάγκην, ἀλλὰ πρὸς ἐντέλειαν τοῦ σχήματος, ἀβελτερία τίς ἐστιν, ὡς Μέγας Βασίλειός φησι. Τὴν ἐκ μεταξῆς κεχρωσμένην ἐσθῆτα, οὐδεὶς ἑαυτῷ περιεβάλλετο, οὐδὲ διαφόρους παγουρίους τοῖς ἱματίοις περιῆπτον· οὐ γὰρ ἤκουον ὡς Οἱ τὰ μαλακὰ φοροῦντες, ἐν τοῖς οἴκοις τῶν βασιλέων εἰσίν.
Ancient Epitome: Bishops and clergymen arraying themselves in splendid clothes and anointed with perfumes must be corrected. Should they persist, they must be punished.
¶163
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Ἐπίσκοποι ἢ κληρικοὶ λαμπραῖς ἐσθήσεσι χρώμενοι, καὶ μύροις, διορθούσθωσαν· εἰ δὲ ἐπιμένοιεν, κολάζεσθωσαν.
That he shall not be allowed to begin the building of an oratory, who has not the means wherewith to finish it.
¶164
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΖ΄
CANON XVII
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ συγχωρεῖσθαι κτίζειν προσευκτήριον οἶκον τὸν μὴ ἔχοντα τὰ πρὸς ἀπαρτισμόν.
Certain monks having left their monasteries because they desired to rule, and, unwilling to obey, are undertaking to build oratories, but have not the means to finish them. Now whoever shall undertake to do anything of this sort, let him be forbidden by the bishop of the place. But if he have the means wherewith to finish, let what he has designed be carried on to completion. The same rule is to be observed with regard to laymen and clerics.
¶165
Τινὲς μοναχοὶ καταλιπόντες τὰ ἑαυτῶν μοναστήρια, καὶ ἄρχειν θέλοντες, καὶ μὴ πειθόμενοι, οἰκοδομοῦσιν εὐκτηρίους οἴκους, καὶ οὐκ ἔχουσιν, πόθεν ἀπαρτίσωσιν. Ὅστις οὖν τοιοῦτόν τι ποιῆσαι βούλοιτο, κωλυέσθω παρὰ τοῦ κατὰ τόπον ἐπισκόπου. Εἰ δὲ ἔχει, πόθεν τελειώσῃ, εἰς τέλος ἀγέσθω τὸ σπουδαζόμενον. Τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ ἐπὶ λαϊκῶν, καὶ κληρικῶν φυλάττεσθαι.
Ancient Epitome: Whoever wishes to build a monastery, if he has the wherewithal to finish it, let him begin the work, and let him bring it to a conclusion. But if not, let him be prohibited by the bishop of the place. The same law shall apply to laymen and monks.
¶166
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Ὁ θέλων μοναστήριον κτίσαι, εἰ μὲν ἔχει τὰ πρὸς ἀπαρτισμὸν, ἀρχέτω τοῦ ἔργου, καὶ τελειούτω· εἰ δὲ μή, κωλυέσθω παρὰ τοῦ ἐπισκόπου τοῦ τόπου. Ὁ αὐτὸς νόμος καὶ ἐπὶ λαϊκῶν, καὶ μοναχῶν.
That women ought not to live in bishops' houses, nor in monasteries of men.
¶167
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΗ΄
CANON XVIII
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν γυναῖκας ἐν ἐπισκοπείοις, ἢ ἀνδρείοις μοναστηρίοις, οἰκεῖν.
Be without offense to those who are without, says the divine Apostle. Now for women to live in Bishops' houses or in monasteries is ground for grave offense. Whoever therefore is known to have a female slave or freewoman in the episcopal palace or in a monastery for the discharge of some service, let him be rebuked. And if he still continue to retain her, let him be deposed. If it happens that women are on the suburban estates, and the bishop or hegumenos desires to go there, so long as the bishop or hegumenos is present, let no woman at that time continue her work, but let her betake herself to some other place until the bishop [or hegumenos] has departed, so that there be no occasion of complaint.
¶168
Ἀνεπίληπτοι τοῖς ἔξωθεν, λέγει ὁ θεῖος Ἀπόστολος. Τὸ δέ γε γυναῖκας ἐν ἐπισκοπείοις, ἢ μοναστηρίοις, οἰκεῖν, μεγάλης ἐστὶ σκάνδαλον ἀφορμή. Δεῖ τοίνυν τὸν γινωσκόμενον, εἴτε δούλην, εἴτε ἐλευθέραν, ἐν τῷ ἐπισκοπείῳ κεκτημένον, ἢ ἐν μοναστηρίῳ, πρός τινα διακονίαν, νουθετεῖσθαι· εἰ δὲ ἐπιμένοι, καθαιρείσθω. Εἰ δὲ συμβαίνει ἐν τοῖς προαστείοις γυναῖκας ὑπάρχειν, καὶ βούλεται ὁ ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ ἡγούμενος, ἐκεῖσε ἀπελθεῖν, ἐπὶ τῷ παρεῖναι ἐπίσκοπον, ἢ ἡγούμενον, διακονεῖν γυναῖκας μηδαμῶς, ἀλλ᾽ εἰς ἕτερον ὑποστέλλεσθαι τόπον, ἕως ἂν ἀναχωρήσῃ ὁ ἐπίσκοπος, ἵνα μὴ γένηται μωμητὸν.
Ancient Epitome: It is not fitting that women should be kept in episcopal houses or in monasteries. If anyone shall dare to do so, he shall be reproved; but if he persists, he shall be deposed. No woman is allowed to serve or even to appear where a bishop or a superior of a monastery is present, but let her keep herself apart until he be gone.
¶169
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Οὐχ ἁρμόζει γυναῖκας ἐν ἐπισκοπείοις ἢ ἐν μοναστηρίοις κατέχεσθαι. Εἰ δὲ τολμῷτό τι, νουθετείσθω· εἰ δὲ ἐπιμένοι, καθαιρείσθω. Γυνὴ οὐκέτι δουλεύειν, ἢ ὁρᾶσθαι ὅπου ἂν ᾖ ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ ἡγούμενος· ἀλλ᾽ ἕως ἂν ἀπέλθῃ, διίστασθαι.
That the vows of those in holy orders and of monks, and of nuns are to be made without the exaction of gifts.
¶170
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΙΘ΄
CANON XIX
Περὶ τοῦ δεῖν ἀδωρήτους γίνεσθαι τὰς χειροτονίας τῶν ἱερωμένων, ἢ μοναζόντων.
The abomination of filthy lucre has made such inroads among the rulers of the churches, that certain of those who call themselves religious men and women, forgetting the commandments of the Lord have been altogether led astray, and for the sake of money have received those presenting themselves for the sacerdotal order and the monastic life. And hence the first step of those so received being unlawful, the whole proceeding is rendered null, as says Basil the Great. For it is not possible that God should be served by means of mammon. If therefore, anyone is found doing anything of this kind, if he be a bishop or hegumenos, or one of the priesthood, either let him cease to do so any longer or else let him be deposed, according to the second canon of the Holy Council of Chalcedon. If the offender be an abbess, let her be sent away from her monastery, and placed in another in a subordinate position. In like manner is a hegumenos to be dealt with, who has not the ordination of a presbyter. With regard to what has been given by parents as a dowry for their children, or which persons themselves have contributed out of their own property, with the declaration that such gifts were made to God, we have decreed, that whether the persons in whose behalf the gifts were made, continue to live in the monastery or not, the gifts are to remain with the monastery in accordance with their first determination; unless indeed there be ground for complaint against the superior.
¶171
Τὸ μύσος τῆς αἰσχροκερδείας, ἐπὶ τοσοῦτον τῶν ἐκκλησιῶν προεστώτων ἐνειργάσατο, ὥστε καί τινας τῶν χριστιανιζόντων, ἀνδρῶν τε καὶ γυναικῶν, λήθην ποιησαμένους τῶν Κυριακῶν ἐντολῶν, παντελῶς διεστράφθαι, ὥστε διὰ δόσεως χρημάτων τοὺς εἰς ἱερατικὸν βαθμόν, ἢ μονάζοντα βίον, προσιόντας δέχεσθαι· καὶ οὕτω παράνομον οὖσαν τὴν ἀρχήν, ἄκυρον ὅλον κατασκευάζεσθαι, καθὼς ὁ μέγας Βασίλειός φησιν. Οὐ γάρ ἐστι Θεὸν δουλεύεσθαι διὰ μαμωνᾶ. Εἴ τις οὖν εὑρεθείη τοῦτο πεποιηκὼς, εἰ μὲν ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ ἡγούμενος, ἢ τῶν ἱερωμένων τίς ἐστιν, ἢ παυέσθω μηκέτι τοῦτο ποιῶν, ἢ καθαιρείσθω, κατὰ τὸν δεύτερον κανόνα τῆς ἁγίας Συνόδου Χαλκηδόνος. Ἡγουμένη δέ, ἀπορριπτέσθω τοῦ μοναστηρίου, καὶ εἰς ἕτερον μοναστήριον τάσσεσθαι ἐν ὑποταγῇ. Ὡσαύτως δὲ γινέσθω κἀπὶ ἡγουμένου, μὴ ἔχοντος πρεσβυτέρου τάξιν. Περὶ δὲ ὧν παρὰ γονέων τοῖς τέκνοις, ἢ καὶ ἑαυτοῖς τινες, προσενέγκαντες, προφάσει δόσεως Θεῷ, καθωσίωσαν, ἔδοξε, κἂν διαμείνωσιν ἐν τοῖς μοναστηρίοις, κἂν μή, παραμένειν αὐτοῖς τὰ τοιαῦτα, κατὰ τὴν ἐξ ἀρχῆς προαίρεσιν, εἰ μὴ ἄρα εἴη αἰτία κατὰ τοῦ προεστῶτος.
Ancient Epitome: Whoever for money admits those coming to Holy Orders or the monastic life, if he be bishop, or superior of a monastery or any other in sacred orders, shall either cease or be deposed. And the Superior of a monastery of women shall be expelled [if she have done so] and shall be given over to subjection. The same shall be the case with a superior of monks, if he be not a priest. But the possessions brought by those who come in, let them remain, whether the persons remain or not, provided the superior be not to blame.
¶172
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Ὁ διὰ χρημάτων δεχόμενος τοὺς ἐπὶ χειροτονίᾳ ἢ μονῇ ἐρχομένους, εἰ μὲν ἐπίσκοπος, ἢ ἡγούμενος, ἢ ἱερωμένος τίς, ἢ παυέσθω, ἢ καθαιρείσθω. Ἡγουμένη δὲ γυναικῶν ἐκβαλλέσθω, καὶ ὑποτάσσεσθαι. Ὡσαύτως κἀπὶ ἡγουμένου μὴ ὄντος πρεσβυτέρου. Τὰ δὲ εἰσενεχθέντα παρὰ τῶν εἰσιόντων, κἂν μείνωσι, κἂν μή, μενέτω, ἂν μὴ ὁ προεστὼς αἴτιος ᾖ.
That from henceforth, no double monastery shall be erected; and concerning the double monasteries already in existence.
¶173
ΚΑΝΩΝ Κ΄
CANON XX
Περὶ τοῦ μηκέτι γίνεσθαι διπλοῦν μοναστήριον· περὶ τῶν ἤδη ὄντων διπλῶν μοναστηρίων.
We decree that from henceforth, no double monastery shall be erected; because this has become an offense and cause of complaint to many. In the case of those persons who with the members of their family propose to leave the world and follow the monastic life, let the men go into a monastery for men, and the women into a monastery for women; for this is well-pleasing to God. The double monasteries which are already in existence, shall observe the rule of our holy Father Basil, and shall be ordered by his precepts, monks and nuns shall not dwell together in the same monastery, for in thus living together adultery finds its occasion. No monk shall have access to a nunnery; nor shall a nun be permitted to enter a monastery for the sake of conversing with anyone therein. No monk shall sleep in a monastery for women, nor eat alone with a nun. When food is brought by men to the canonesses, let the abbess accompanied by some one of the aged nuns, receive it outside the gates of the women's monastery. When a monk desires to see one of his kinswomen, who may be in the nunnery, let him converse with her in the presence of the abbess, and that in a very few words, and then let him speedily take his departure.
¶174
Ὁρίζομεν, ἀπὸ τοῦ νῦν διπλοῦν μοναστήριον μὴ γίνεσθαι· τοῦτο γὰρ πολλοῖς γέγονεν ἀφορμὴ σκανδάλου καὶ γογγυσμοῦ. Τοὺς δὲ σὺν τοῖς αὐτῶν ἰδίοις ἀναχωρεῖν ἐπιτηδεύοντας τοῦ βίου, καὶ τὸν μοναχικὸν ζῆλον κατὰ πρόθεσιν μεταδιώκειν, τοὺς μὲν ἄνδρας εἰς ἀνδρεῖον μοναστήριον, τὰς δὲ γυναῖκας εἰς γυναικεῖον μοναστήριον χωρεῖν· οὕτω γὰρ τῷ Θεῷ εὐάρεστον. Τὰ δὲ ὄντα διπλᾶ μοναστήρια, κατὰ τὸν κανόνα τοῦ ἁγίου Πατρὸς ἡμῶν Βασιλείου, διατυπουμένω, ταῖς αὐτοῦ διατάξεσι στοιχείτω. Μὴ συνοικείτωσαν μοναχοὶ καὶ μοναχαὶ ἐν ἑνὶ μοναστηρίῳ· ἐπιμιξία γὰρ μοιχείας πρόφασιν παρέχει. Μὴ παρρησιαζέσθω μοναχὸς πρὸς μοναχήν· μηδὲ μοναχὴ πρὸς μοναχόν, ἰδιοπραγούσης τῆς πρὸς τὸν ἕτερον εἰσόδου. Μὴ κοιμάσθω μοναχὸς ἐν γυναικείῳ μοναστηρίῳ· μηδὲ μόνην μοναχὴν τρώγειν. Ὅτε δὲ παραφέροιτο τὰ ἀναγκαῖα παρὰ ἀνδρῶν, ἔξω τῆς πύλης τοῦ γυναικείου μοναστηρίου δέχεσθαι ταῦτα τὴν ἡγουμένην, μετά τινος τῶν γεραιτέρων μοναχῶν. Εἰ δὲ βούλοιτο συγγενῆ αὐτὸν ἰδεῖν μοναχός, προσλαλούντων αὐτῷ, τῆς ἡγουμένης παρούσης, μετὰ δύο ἢ τριῶν εὐλαβεστέρων μοναχῶν· καὶ διὰ βραχέων ταῦτα λεγέσθω, καὶ ταχέως διὰ ταύτης ἀπεμπολιζέσθω.
Ancient Epitome: Monasteries shall not be double, neither shall monks and nuns live in the same building, nor shall they talk together apart. Moreover if a man takes anything to a canoness, let him wait without and hand it to her, and let him see his relative in the presence of her superior.
¶175
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Μοναστήρια μὴ εἶναι διπλᾶ, μήτε ἄνδρες καὶ γυναῖκες ἐν τῷ αὐτῷ κτίσματι οἰκείτωσαν, μηδὲ μόνων πρὸς μόνους λαλείτωσαν. Εἰ δὲ προσφέρῃ τις ταῖς κανονικαῖς, ἔξωθεν αὐτὰς δέχεσθαι· ἢ συγγενῆ ἰδεῖν βούλοιτο, ἐνώπιον τῆς ἡγουμένης ἰδέτω.
That monks are not to leave their monasteries and go into others.
¶176
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΚΑ΄
CANON XXI
Περὶ τοῦ μὴ δεῖν μοναχοὺς καταλιμπάνειν τὰ ἑαυτῶν μοναστήρια, καὶ εἰς ἕτερα ἀπέρχεσθαι.
A monk or nun ought not to leave the monastery to which he or she is attached, and betake themselves to others. But if one do this, he ought to be received as a guest. It is not however proper that he be made a member of the monastery, without the consent of his hegumenos.
¶177
Μοναχὸν, ἢ μοναχὴν, μὴ ἐξίστασθαι τοῦ οἰκείου μοναστηρίου, καὶ εἰς ἕτερα ἀπέρχεσθαι. Ἐὰν δὲ γένηται τοῦτο, ξενοδοχεῖσθαι αὐτὸν προσήκει. Οὐ μὴν συσταθῆναι αὐτῷ προσήκει, χωρὶς γνώμης τοῦ ἡγουμένου.
Ancient Epitome: It is not allowed to a monk or a nun to leave her own house and enter another; but if he (or she) enters let (him or her) be received as a guest; but let him (or her) not be admitted at all nor given hospitality contrary to the will of the superior.
¶178
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Μὴ ἐξέστω μοναχῷ ἢ μοναχῇ, τὸν ἴδιον οἶκον ἀπολιπόντας, εἰς ἕτερον ἀπέρχεσθαι. Ἐὰν δὲ γένηται, ξενοδοχείσθω· μὴ δὲ συσταθῆναι αὐτῷ, μηδὲ ξενοδοχηθῆναι παρὰ γνώμην τοῦ ἡγουμένου.
That when it happens that monks have to eat with women they ought to observe giving of thanks, and abstemiousness, and discretion.
¶179
ΚΑΝΩΝ ΚΒ΄
CANON XXII
Περὶ τοῦ ὅταν συμβῇ μοναχοὺς μετὰ γυναικῶν τρώγειν, εὐχαριστίας καὶ ἐγκρατείας καὶ σωφροσύνης μετέχειν.
To surrender all things to God, and not to serve our own wills, is great gain. For says the divine Apostle, whether you eat or drink, do all to the glory of God. And Christ our God has bidden us in his Gospels, to cut off the beginning of sins; for not only is adultery rebuked by him, but even the movement of the mind towards the act of adultery when he says, Whosoever looks on a woman to lust after her, has committed adultery with her already in his heart. We who have been thus taught ought therefore to purify our minds. Now although all things are lawful, all things are not expedient, as we have been taught by the mouth of the Apostle. It is needful that all men should eat in order that they may live. And for those to whom life consists of marrying, and bringing forth children, and of the condition of the lay state, there is nothing unbecoming in men and women eating together, only let them give thanks to the giver of the food; but if there be the entertainments of the theatre, that is, Satanic songs accompanied with the meretricious inflections of harps, there come upon them, through these things, the curse of the prophet, who thus speaks: Woe to them who drink wine with harp and psaltery, but they regard not the works of the Lord, and consider not the works of his hands. Whenever persons of this sort are found among Christians, let them amend their ways; but if they will not do so, let there overtake them the penalties which have been enacted in the canons by our predecessors. With regard to those whose life is free from care and apart from men, that is, those who have resolved before the Lord God to carry the solitary yoke, they should sit down alone and in silence. Moreover it is also altogether unlawful for those who have chosen the priestly life to eat in private with women, unless it be with God-fearing and discreet men and women, so that even their feast may be turned to spiritual edification. The same rule is to be observed with relatives. Again, if it happen that a monk or priest while on a journey does not have with him what is absolutely necessary for him, and, because of his pressing needs, thinks well to turn aside into an inn or into someone's house, this he is permitted to do, seeing that need compels.
¶180
Πάντα Θεῷ ἀνατιθέναι, μηδὲ τοῖς ἰδίοις ἐξυπηρετεῖσθαι θελήμασι, λυσιτέλεια μεγάλη. Φησὶν γὰρ ὁ θεῖος Ἀπόστολος· Εἴτε ἐσθίετε, εἴτε πίνετε, εἴτε τι ποιεῖτε, πάντα εἰς δόξαν Θεοῦ ποιεῖτε. Καὶ Χριστὸς δὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν ἐν τοῖς Εὐαγγελίοις κελεύει, τῇ τομῇ τὴν ἀρχὴν τῶν ἁμαρτημάτων ἀφανίζειν· οὐ μόνον γὰρ μοιχεία παῤ αὐτοῦ ἐλέγχεται, ἀλλὰ καὶ ἡ τῆς διανοίας πρὸς τὴν μοιχείαν κίνησις, λέγοντος· Ὁ βλέπων γυναῖκα πρὸς τὸ ἐπιθυμῆσαι αὐτῆς, ἤδη ἐμοίχευσεν αὐτὴν ἐν τῇ καρδίᾳ αὐτοῦ. Ἡμεῖς τοίνυν οὕτω πεπαιδευμένοι, καθαίρειν ὀφείλομεν τὰς ἐννοίας. Εἰ καὶ πάντα ἔξεστιν, ἀλλ᾽ οὐ πάντα συμφέρει, διδασκόμενοι παρὰ τοῦ Ἀποστόλου. Πρέπει πάντας ἀνθρώπους ἐσθίειν, ἵνα ζῶσι· καὶ τούτοις μὲν οἷς τὸ ζῆν γάμος, τεκνογονία, βίος δημόσιος, οὐδὲν ἀπᾴδον συνεστιᾶσθαι ἄνδρας καὶ γυναῖκας, μετὰ εὐχαριστίας τῷ τὰ ἐδώδιμα δεδωρότι Θεῷ· ἠθῶν δὲ θεατρικῶν, δαιμονικῶν ᾀσμάτων, παραπεμπομένων ταῖς πορνικαῖς τῶν κρουμάτων ἐμμελείαις, διὰ τούτων ἐπέρχεται αὐτοῖς ἡ τοῦ προφήτου κατάρα λέγοντος· Οὐαὶ οἱ πίνοντες τὸν οἶνον μετὰ κιθάρας καὶ ψαλτηρίου, τὰ δὲ ἔργα Κυρίου οὐκ ἐμβλέπουσι, καὶ τὰ ποιήματα τῶν χειρῶν αὐτοῦ οὐ κατανοοῦσιν. Ὅπου ἂν οὖν εὑρεθῶσι τοιοῦτοι ἐν Χριστιανοῖς, διορθούσθωσαν· εἰ δὲ μή, κρατείτωσαν κατ᾽ αὐτῶν αἱ παρὰ τῶν προφθασάντων ἐν τοῖς κανόσιν ἡμῖν ἐκτεθεῖσαι ἐπιτιμήσεις. Τοὺς δὲ ἐχούσῃ ζωὴν μεμεριμνημένην καὶ ἠρεμοῦσαν, καὶ τοὺς πρὸ τοῦ Κυρίου Θεοῦ διαγνόντας, τὸν μονήρη ζυγὸν ἀναδέξασθαι, μόνους καθεζομένους σιωπᾶν. Ἔτι μὴν καὶ τοῖς τὸν ἱερατικὸν ἀπολεξαμένοις βίον, παντελῶς μὴ συγχωρεῖσθαι, ἰδικῶς μετὰ γυναικῶν τρώγειν, εἰ μή ποτε σὺν θεοσεβέσι καὶ εὐλαβέσι, συναμφότεροι, ἵνα καὶ τὸ συμπόσιον εἰς πνευματικὴν ὠφέλειαν γένηται. Τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ ἐπὶ συγγενῶν φυλάττεσθαι. Πάλιν, εἰ συμβαίη μοναχὸν, ἢ ἱερωμένον, ὁδοιποροῦντα, μὴ ἔχειν τὰ πρὸς χρείαν ἀναγκαῖα, κατεπείγοντος χάριν, βούλοιτο εἰς πανδοχεῖον, ἢ οἶκόν τινος, γενέσθαι, συγκεχώρηται τοῦτο, χρείας γὰρ καὶ ἀνάγκης τὸ πρᾶγμα.
Ancient Epitome: There is no objection to laywomen eating with men: it is not right however for men who have chosen the lonely life, to eat privately with women; unless perchance together with them that fear God and with religious men and women. But when travelling, a monk or anyone in sacred orders, not carrying necessary provisions with him, may enter a public house.
¶181
Παλαιὰ Ἐπιτομή: Κοσμικαῖς γυναιξὶν ἁρμόζει μετὰ ἀνδρῶν τρώγειν· οὐκ ὀρθῶς δὲ τοὺς ἀποταξαμένους μόνους μετὰ γυναικῶν τρώγειν· εἰ μή που μετὰ θεοσεβῶν, καὶ συναμφοτέρων. Ὁδοιπορῶν δὲ μοναχὸς, ἢ ἱερωμένος, μὴ βαστάζων ἐπιτήδεια, εἰς πανδοχεῖον εἰσίτω.
To our most religious and most serene princes, Constantine and Irene his mother. Tarasius, the unworthy bishop of your God-protected royal city, new Rome, and all the holy Council which met at the good pleasure of God and upon the command of your Christ-loving majesty in the renowned metropolis of Nicaea, the second council to assemble in this city.
¶182
ETTER OF THE SYNOD TO THE EMPERORS CONSTANTINE AND IRENE
Η ΕΠΙΣΤΟΛΗ ΤΗΣ ΣΥΝΟΔΟΥ ΠΡΟΣ ΤΟΥΣ ΒΑΣΙΛΕΙΣ
THE LETTER OF THE SYNOD TO THE EMPEROR AND EMPRESS
Τοῖς εὐσεβεστάτοις καὶ γαληνιωτάτοις ἡμῶν δεσπόταις, Κωνσταντίνῳ καὶ Εἰρήνῃ τῇ μητρὶ αὐτοῦ. Ταράσιος, ὁ ἀνάξιος ἐπίσκοπος τῆς θεοφυλάκτου ὑμῶν βασιλίδος νέας Ῥώμης, καὶ πᾶσα ἡ ἁγία σύνοδος, ἡ θελήσει Θεοῦ καὶ κελεύσει τῆς φιλοχρίστου ὑμῶν βασιλείας συναχθεῖσα ἐν τῇ περιωνύμῳ μητροπόλει Νικαίᾳ, δευτέρως ἐν αὐτῇ συγκροτηθεῖσα.
Christ our God (who is the head of the Church) was glorified, most noble princes, when your heart, which he holds in his hands, gave forth that good word bidding us to assemble in his name, in order that we might strengthen our hold on the sure, immovable, and God-given truth contained in the Church's dogmas. As your heads were crowned with gold and most brilliant stones, so likewise were your minds adorned with the precepts of the Gospel and the teachings of the Fathers. And being the disciples and companions, as it were, of those whose sounds went forth into all the earth, you became the leaders in the way of piety of all who bore the name of Christ, setting forth clearly the word of truth, and giving a brilliant example of orthodoxy and piety; so that you were to the faithful as so many burning lamps. The Church which was ready to fall, you upheld with your hands, strengthening it with sound doctrine, and bringing into the unity of a right judgment those who were at variance. We may therefore well say with boldness that it was through you that the good pleasure of God brought about the triumph of godliness, and filled our mouth with joy and our tongue with gladness. And these things our lips utter with a formal decree. For what is more glorious than to maintain the Church's interests; and what else is more calculated to provoke our gladness?
¶183
Χριστὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, ὃς κεφαλὴ τῆς ἐκκλησίας ὑπάρχει, δεδόξασται, κράτιστοι δεσπόται, ἐπεὶ ἡ ὑμῶν καρδία, ἣν κατέχει ἐν ταῖς ἑαυτοῦ χερσίν, προέβαλε τὸν καλὸν λόγον, κελεύσασα ἡμᾶς συναχθῆναι εἰς τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ, ὅπως κρατύνωμεν τὴν ἀσφαλῆ καὶ ἀκλόνητον καὶ θεοπαράδοτον ἀλήθειαν τῶν ἐκκλησιαστικῶν δογμάτων. Ὥσπερ γὰρ αἱ κεφαλαὶ ὑμῶν χρυσῷ καὶ λίθοις διαυγεστάτοις ἐστεφάνωνται, οὕτως καὶ οἱ ὑμέτεροι λογισμοὶ τοῖς εὐαγγελικοῖς καὶ πατρικοῖς μαθήμασι κεκοσμήνται. Καὶ ὡς μαθηταὶ καὶ συνακόλουθοι, μικροῦ δεῖν, ἐκείνων ὧν ὁ φθόγγος εἰς πᾶσαν τὴν γῆν ἐξῆλθε, γεγονότες, ἡγεμόνες τῆς εὐσεβείας παντὸς τοῦ χριστωνύμου γένους κατέστητε, τρανῶς προβαλλόμενοι τὸν λόγον τῆς ἀληθείας, λαμπρὸν παράδειγμα ὀρθοδοξίας τε καὶ εὐσεβείας παρεχόμενοι· ὥστε τυχεῖν ὑμᾶς τῶν πιστῶν, ὡς λύχνους καιομένους. Τὴν γὰρ μέλλουσαν πεσεῖν ἐκκλησίαν, ταῖς ὑμετέραις χερσὶν ἀνωρθώσατε, τοῖς ὀρθοῖς δόγμασι ταύτην κρατύνοντες, καὶ τοὺς διεστηκότας εἰς ἕνωσιν ὀρθῆς γνώμης συνάγοντες. Διὸ μετὰ παρρησίας λέγειν εὖ ἕξομεν, ὅτι δι᾽ ὑμῶν ἡ εὐδοκία τοῦ Θεοῦ κατώρθωσε τὰ τῆς εὐσεβείας τρόπαια, καὶ ἐνέπλησε χαρᾶς τὸ στόμα ἡμῶν, καὶ τὴν γλῶσσαν ἡμῶν ἀγαλλιάσεως. Ταῦτα δὲ τὰ ῥήματα, καὶ ψήφῳ τρανῶς ἐκστομίζομεν. Τί γὰρ τούτου ἐνδοξότερον, τοῦ τὰ τῆς ἐκκλησίας φρονεῖν; Ἢ τί ἕτερον ποιῆσαι εὔκαιρον τὴν ἡμετέραν πρόξενον ἀγαλλιάσεως;
Certain men rose up, having the form of godliness, inasmuch as they were clothed with the dignity of the priesthood, but denying the power thereof; and thus deserving for themselves the charge of being but priests of Babylon. Of such the word of prophecy had before declared that lawlessness went forth from the priests of Babylon. Nay more, they banded themselves together in a sanhedrim, like to that which Caiaphas held, and became the propagators of ungodly doctrines. And having a mouth full of cursing and bitterness, they thought to win the mastery by means of abusive words. With a slanderous tongue and a pen of a like character, and objecting to the very terms used by God himself, they devised marvellous tales, and then proceeded to stigmatise as idolaters the royal priesthood and the holy nation, even those who had put on Christ, and by his grace had been kept safe from the folly of idols. And having a mind set upon evil, they took in hand unlawful deeds, thinking to suppress altogether the depicting of the venerable images. Accordingly, as many icons as were set in mosaic work they dug out, and those which were in painted waxwork, they scraped away; thus turning the comely beauty of the sacred temples into complete disorder. Among doings of this sort, it is to be specially noted that the pictures set up on tablets in memory of Christ our God and of his Saints, they gave over to the flames. Finally, in a word, having desecrated our churches, they reduced them to utter confusion. Then some bishops became the leaders of this heresy and where before was peace, they fomented strife among the people; and instead of wheat sowed tares in the Church's fields. They mingled wine with water, and gave the foul draught to those about them. Although but Arabian wolves, they hid themselves under sheeps' clothing, and by specious reasoning against the truth sought to commend their lie. But all the while they hatched asps' eggs and wove a spider's web, as says the prophet; and he that would eat of their eggs, having crushed one, found it to be addled, with a basilisk within it, and giving forth a deadly stench.
¶184
Ἀνέστησάν τινες ἄνθρωποι, μορφὴν μὲν εὐσεβείας ἔχοντες, ἐπείπερ τῇ τῆς ἱερωσύνης περιβέβληνται ἀξίᾳ, τὴν δὲ δύναμιν αὐτῆς ἀρνούμενοι, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο ἑαυτοῖς τὴν κατηγορίαν περιάπτοντες, Βαβυλωνίων εἶναι μόνων ἱερεῖς. Περὶ ὧν ὁ προφήτης πάλαι διαμαρτυρόμενος ἔλεγεν, ὅτι ἀνομία ἐκ Βαβυλῶνος ἐξελεύσεται ἀπὸ πρεσβυτέρων κριτῶν, οἳ ἔδοξαν ἄρχειν. Καὶ μὴν συνελθόντες καθάπερ συνέδριον κατὰ τὴν Καϊάφα σύνοδον, γεγόνασι τῆς ἀσεβείας προστάται. Καὶ στόμα ἔχοντες γέμον ἀρᾶς καὶ πικρίας, ἐν λοιδορίαις κατεκρατεῖν ἐνόμιζον. Γλώσσῃ τε διαβλήματι, καὶ καλάμῳ τοιούτῳ, καὶ ταῖς τοῦ Θεοῦ φωναῖς ἀντιπεσεῖν ἑλόμενοι, τερατείας διεξῆλθον, εἶτα βασιλεῖον ἱεράτευμα, τὸ ἔθνος τὸ ἅγιον, τοὺς ἐνδυσαμένους Χριστὸν, καὶ τῇ τούτου χάριτι φυλαχθέντας καθαροὺς τῆς τῶν εἰδώλων μανίας, εἰδωλολάτρας προσηγόρευσαν. Καὶ νοῦν ἔχοντες μεμελετηκότα κακὰ, χεῖρας εἰς παράνομα διανεστήκασι, δῆθεν πᾶσαν ἱστορίαν σεπτῶν εἰκόνων ἀφανίζειν θέλοντες. Ὅσαι μὲν οὖν ἐκ πολυτελείας ψηφίδος ἀνέκειντο εἰκόνες, κατεσκάφησαν· ὅσαι δὲ ἐκ χρωμάτων, περιολισθήσαντες, ἀφανεῖς κατέστησαν. Καὶ οὕτω τὸ εὐπρεπὲς τῶν ἱερῶν οἴκων εἰς ἀσχημοσύνην περιέστησαν. Καὶ τοῦτο δή ἐστι καινόν, καὶ τὰς ἐν πλαξὶν ἀναστήλους εἰκόνας δεικνυμένας, εἰς μνήμην Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ ἡμῶν, καὶ τῶν τούτου ἁγίων, πυρὶ παραδεδώκασι. Τέλος δὲ πάντων, τὰς ἐκκλησίας βεβηλώσαντες, ἠχρείωσαν. Εἶτα προστῆναι τῆς αἱρέσεως ταύτης ἐπίσκοποι γεγόνασι, καὶ οὗ πρότερον εἰρήνη ἦν, νῦν στάσεις ἐξῆφθον τοῖς λαοῖς· καὶ ἀντὶ πυροῦ ζιζάνια κατέσπειραν τοῖς τῆς ἐκκλησίας γηπέδοις. Ἔμισγον οἶνον ὕδατι, καὶ τοῖς πέλας τὸ μοχθηρὸν πόμα προετίθεσαν. Λύκοι Ἀράβιοι τυγχάνοντες, ἐν ἐνδύμασι προβάτων ἐκρύπτοντο, καὶ πιθανοῖς λόγοις κατὰ τῆς ἀληθείας, τὸ ψεῦδος συστῆσαι ἐπεχείρησαν. Ὠὰ ἀσπίδων ἔθρυπτον, καὶ ἱστὸν ἀράχνης ὕφαινον, κατὰ τὸν προφήτην· καὶ ὁ μέλλων ἐσθίειν τῶν ὠῶν αὐτῶν, συντρίψας εὕρισκε ῥητὸν, καὶ ἐν αὐτῷ βασιλίσκον, ἀποπνέοντα θανατηφόρον ὀδμήν.
In such a state of affairs, with a lie busy destroying the truth, you, most gracious and most noble princes, did not idly allow so grave a plague, and such soul-destroying error long to continue in your day. But moved by the divine Spirit which abides in you, you set yourselves with all your strength utterly to exterminate it, and thus preserve the stability of the Church's government, and likewise concord among your subjects; so that your whole empire might be established in peace agreeably with the name you bear. You rightly reasoned, that it was not to be patiently endured, that while in other matters we could be of one mind and live in concord, yet in what ought to be the chief concern of our life, the peace of the Churches, there was among us strife and division. And that too, when Christ being our head, we ought to be members one of another, and one body, by our mutual agreement and faith. Accordingly, you commanded our holy and numerously-attended council to assemble in the metropolis of Nicaea, in order that after having rid the Church of division, we might restore to unity the separated members, and might be careful to rend and utterly destroy the coarse cloak of false doctrine, which they had woven of thorn fibre, and unfold again the fair robe of orthodoxy.
¶185
Τούτων οὕτως ἐχόντων, τοῦ ψεύδους τὴν ἀλήθειαν λυμαινομένου, οὐ ῥᾳθύμως τὴν τηλικαύτην νόσον, καὶ τὴν οὕτως ὀλέθριον πλάνην, ἐπὶ πολὺ συγκεχωρήκατε διαμεῖναι, γαληνιώτατοι καὶ κράτιστοι δεσπόται· ἀλλὰ τῷ ἐνυπάρχοντι ὑμῖν θείῳ Πνεύματι κινούμενοι, πάσῃ δυνάμει εἰς παντελῆ ταύτης ἐξολέθρευσιν ἑαυτοὺς ἀνειλήφατε, καὶ τῆς μὲν ἐκκλησίας τὴν εὐταξίαν, τῶν ὑπηκόων δὲ τὴν ὁμόνοιαν κρατύνατε, ὅπως πᾶσα ἡ ὑμετέρα βασιλεία τῇ εἰρήνῃ, καὶ τῷ κατ᾽ ἐπωνυμίαν πρέποντι βεβαίως κρατύνηται. Καλῶς γοῦν λογισάμενοι, μὴ φορητὸν εἶναι, τῶν ἄλλων πραγμάτων κοινῇ γνώμῃ καὶ ὁμονοίᾳ διοικουμένων, ἐν ᾧπέρ ἐστι κεφάλαιον τοῦ ζῆν ἡμῖν, τῇ εἰρήνῃ τῶν ἐκκλησιῶν, στάσεις καὶ διαιρέσεις εἶναι, Χριστοῦ κεφαλῆς ὄντος, ἡμᾶς δὲ μέλη ἀλλήλων, καὶ ἓν σῶμα, κατὰ τὴν πρὸς ἀλλήλους συμφωνίαν τε καὶ πίστιν, τυγχάνειν, τὴν ἁγίαν ἡμῶν καὶ πολυάνθρωπον σύνοδον ἐν τῇ μητροπόλει Νικαίᾳ συγκροτηθῆναι κελεύετε, ἵνα, καθάψαντες τῆς ἐκκλησίας τὰς διαιρέσεις, τὰ μερισθέντα μέλη εἰς ἕνωσιν ἀγάγωμεν, καὶ τὸν τραχὺν χιτῶνα τῆς κακοδοξίας, ὃν ἐκ ῥαμνίδων ὕφηναν, διαῤῥῆξαι παντελῶς φροντίσωμεν, καὶ τὸν εὐπρεπῆ χιτῶνα τῆς ὀρθοδοξίας ἀναπτύξωμεν.
And now having carefully traced the traditions of the Apostles and Fathers, we are bold to speak. Having but one mind by the inbreathing of the most Holy Spirit, and being all knit together in one, and understanding the harmonious tradition of the Catholic Church, we are in perfect harmony with the symphonies set forth by the six, holy and ecumenical councils; and accordingly we have anathematised the madness of Arius, the frenzy of Macedonius, the senseless understanding of Apollinarius, the man-worship of Nestorius, the irreverent mingling of the natures devised by Eutyches and Dioscorus, and the many-headed hydra which is their companion. We have also anathematised the idle tales of Origen, Didymus, and Evagrius; and the doctrine of one will held by Sergius, Honorius, Cyrus, and Pyrrhus, or rather, we have anathematised their own evil will. Finally, taught by the Spirit, from whom we have drawn pure water, we have with one accord and one soul, altogether wiped out with the sponge of the divine dogmas the newly devised heresy, well-worthy to be classed with those just mentioned, which springing up after them, uttered such empty nonsense about the sacred icons. And the contrivers of this vain, but revolutionary babbling we have cast forth far from the Church's precincts.
¶186
Καὶ δὴ τὰς τῶν Ἀποστόλων καὶ Πατέρων παραδόσεις ἀκριβώσαντες, μετὰ παρρησίας λαλοῦμεν. Μιᾷ γὰρ γνώμῃ, κατὰ ἐμφύσημα τοῦ ἁγιωτάτου Πνεύματος, γενόμενοι, καὶ πάντες συναρμολογούμενοι, καὶ τὴν σύμφωνον παράδοσιν τῆς καθολικῆς ἐκκλησίας νοοῦντες, ταῖς ἐκτεθείσαις συμφωνίαις τῶν ἓξ ἁγίων καὶ οἰκουμενικῶν συνόδων κατὰ πάντα στοιχοῦμεν· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο Ἀρείου μὲν τὴν μανίαν, Μακεδονίου δὲ τὴν λύσσαν, Ἀπολιναρίου δὲ τὴν ἀσύνετον διάνοιαν, Νεστορίου δὲ τὴν ἀνθρωπολατρείαν, Εὐτυχοῦς τε καὶ Διοσκόρου τὴν μὴ εὐπρεπῆ κρᾶσιν, καὶ τὴν συμπαρεπομένην αὐτοῖς πολυκέφαλον ὕδραν, ἀνεθεματίσαμεν. Ὠριγένους τε, Διδύμου, Εὐαγρίου τὰς φλυαρίας, καὶ Σεργίου, Ὁνωρίου, Κύρου, Πύρρου, τὸ μονοθέλητον δόγμα, μᾶλλον δὲ κατειργάσαμεν τῆς αὐτῶν κακῆς προαιρέσεως. Τέλος, ὑπὸ τοῦ Πνεύματος χειραγωγούμενοι, παρ᾽ οὗ τὰ καθαρὰ τῶν ὑδάτων ὠμόσαμεν, μιᾷ γνώμῃ καὶ μιᾷ ψυχῇ, τὴν νεοφυῆ ταύτην, ταῖς προλαβούσαις συγκαταριθμουμένην, μετ᾽ ἐκείνας φυεῖσαν αἵρεσιν, κενοφωνοῦσαν περὶ σεπτῶν εἰκόνων, τῷ σπόγγῳ τῶν θείων δογμάτων παντελῶς ἐξαλείψαντες, τοὺς ταύτης ἐπινοητὰς, τῆς ματαίας, ἀλλὰ καὶ ταραχώδους φλυαρίας, πόρρω τῆς ἐκκλησίας ἐξεβάλομεν.
And as the hands and feet are moved in accordance with the directions of the mind, so likewise, we, having received the grace and strength of the Spirit, and having also the assistance and co-operation of your royal authority, have with one voice declared as piety and proclaimed as truth: that the sacred icons of our Lord Jesus Christ are to be had and retained, inasmuch as he was very man; also those which set forth what is historically narrated in the Gospels; and those which represent our undefiled Lady, the holy Mother of God; and likewise those of the Holy Angels (for they have manifested themselves in human form to those who were counted worthy of the vision of them), or of any of the Saints. [We have also decreed] that the brave deeds of the Saints be portrayed on tablets and on the walls, and upon the sacred vessels and vestments, as has been the custom of the holy Catholic Church of God from ancient times; which custom was regarded as having the force of law in the teaching both of those holy leaders who lived in the first ages of the Church, and also of their successors our reverend Fathers. [We have likewise decreed] that these images are to be reverenced, that is, salutations are to be offered to them. The reason for using the word is, that it has a two-fold signification. For κυνεῖν in the old Greek tongue signifies both to salute and to kiss. And the preposition πρός gives to it the additional idea of strong desire towards the subject; as for example, we have φέρω and προσφέρω, κυρῶ and προσκυρῶ, and so also we have κυνέω and προσκυνέω. Which last word implies salutation and strong love; for that which one loves he also reverences and what he reverences that he greatly loves, as the everyday custom, which we observe towards those we love, bears witness, and in which both ideas are practically illustrated when two friends meet together. The word is not only made use of by us, but we also find it set down in the Divine Scriptures by the ancients. For it is written in the histories of the Kings, And David rose up and fell upon his face and did reverence to Jonathan three times and kissed him. And what is it that the Lord in the Gospel says concerning the Pharisees? They love the uppermost rooms at feasts and greetings in the markets. It is evident that by greetings here, he means reverence, for the Pharisees being very high-minded and thinking themselves to be righteous were eager to be reverenced by all, but not [merely] to be kissed. For to receive salutations of this latter sort savoured too much of lowly humility, and this was not to the Pharisees' liking. We have also the example of Paul the divine Apostle, as Luke in the Acts of the Apostles relates: When we had come to Jerusalem, the brethren received us gladly, and the day following Paul went in with us unto James, and all the presbyters were present. And when he had saluted them, he declared particularly what things God had wrought among the Gentiles by his ministry. By the salutation here mentioned, the Apostle evidently intended to render that reverence of honour which we show to one another, and of which he speaks when he says concerning Jacob, that he reverenced the top of his staff. With these examples agrees what Gregory surnamed Theologus says: Honour Bethlehem, and reverence the manger.
¶187
Καὶ καθάπερ χεῖρες καὶ πόδες κατὰ νεύσεις λογισμοῦ κινοῦνται, οὕτω δὴ καὶ ἡμεῖς, τήν τε τοῦ Πνεύματος χάριν καὶ δύναμιν δεξάμενοι, τήν τε συμμαχίαν καὶ συλλήπτορα τῆς βασιλικῆς ὑμῶν ἐξουσίας τὴν συνεργίαν προσλαβόμενοι, μιᾷ φωνῇ εὐσεβὲς ἐφεξῆς καὶ ἀληθὲς ἐκηρύξαμεν· εἶναι τε καὶ κρατεῖν τὰς σεπτὰς εἰκόνας τοῦ Κυρίου ἡμῶν Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ, ὡς ἀληθῶς ἐνανθρωπήσαντος· εἶναι τε τὰς ἐκ τῶν εὐαγγελικῶν ἱστορημάτων· εἶναι τε τὰς τῆς ἀχράντου δεσποίνης ἡμῶν, τῆς ἁγίας Θεοτόκου· εἶναι τε τὰς τῶν ἁγίων Ἀγγέλων (ἐφάνησαν γὰρ καὶ ἀνθρωπίνως τοῖς καταξιωθεῖσι τῆς πρὸς αὐτοὺς ὁπτασίας)· εἶναι τε πάντων τῶν ἁγίων. Καὶ τὰς τῶν ἁγίων πράξεις, ἔν τε σανίσι καὶ τοίχοις, καὶ ἱεροῖς σκεύεσι καὶ ἐνδύμασι, τυποῦσθαι, καθὼς ἔθος ἦν τῇ ἁγίᾳ καθολικῇ τοῦ Θεοῦ ἐκκλησίᾳ ἐκ παλαιοῦ· ὃ παρὰ τοῖς ἁγίοις ἐκείνοις ἀνδράσι, καὶ προκαθηγησαμένοις αὐτῆς, καὶ τοῖς μετ᾽ αὐτοὺς σεπτοῖς Πατράσι, νόμου δύναμιν, νομίζεται. Ταύτας τε προσκυνεῖν, τουτέστι, ἀσπασμὸν αὐταῖς προσφέρειν. Τοῦ δὲ λόγου τὴν αἰτίαν, διότι διττός ἐστι· τὸ γὰρ κυνεῖν, ἀσπασμός ἐστι κατὰ τὴν παλαιὰν Ἑλλήνων φωνήν· ἡ δὲ πρὸς πρόθεσις, τὸν εἰς πρόσωπον ἰσχυρὸν ἔρωτα παρίστησι· καθάπερ φέρω καὶ προσφέρω, κυρῶ καὶ προσκυρῶ· τρόπον τοιοῦτον, κυνέω καὶ προσκυνέω. Τὸ γὰρ προσκυνέω, ἀσπασμόν τέ ἐστι, καὶ σφοδρὸν ἔρωτα· ὃ γὰρ ἀγαπᾷ τις, τοῦτο καὶ προσκυνεῖ, καὶ ὃ προσκυνεῖ, σφόδρα ἀγαπᾷ, ὡς καὶ ἡ καθ᾽ ἑκάστην ἡμέραν πρὸς τοὺς φιλουμένους μαρτυρεῖ συνήθεια, ἐν ᾗ καὶ τὰ πράγματα δείκνυται, φίλων συναντώντων ἀλλήλοις. Ἡ δὲ φωνὴ αὕτη οὐ μόνον παρ᾽ ἡμῶν γίνεται, ἀλλὰ κειμένην αὐτὴν εὕρομεν καὶ ἐν τῇ θείᾳ Γραφῇ παρὰ τῶν παλαιῶν. Γέγραπται γὰρ ἐν ταῖς Βασιλείαις· Καὶ ἀνέστη Δαυὶδ, καὶ πεσὼν ἐπὶ πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ, προσεκύνησε τῷ Ἰωνάθαν τρὶς, καὶ κατεφίλησεν αὐτόν. Τί δέ ἐστιν ὃ ἐν τῷ Εὐαγγελίῳ περὶ Φαρισαίων ὁ Κύριός φησιν; Φιλοῦσι τὰς πρωτοκλισίας ἐν τοῖς δείπνοις, καὶ τοὺς ἀσπασμοὺς ἐν ταῖς ἀγοραῖς. Δῆλον οὖν, ὅτι ἀσπασμὸν ἐνταῦθα, τὴν προσκύνησιν λέγει· μεγαλόφρονες γὰρ ὄντες οἱ Φαρισαῖοι, καὶ δίκαιοι ἑαυτοὺς νομίζοντες, προσκυνεῖσθαι πρὸς πάντων ἤθελον, οὐ μὴν καταφιλεῖσθαι, ταπεινὸν γὰρ πάνυ τοῦτο, καὶ οὐκ ἤθελον Φαρισαῖοι. Ἔχομεν δὲ καὶ Παύλου τοῦ θείου Ἀποστόλου, Λουκᾶς ἐν ταῖς Πράξεσι τῶν Ἀποστόλων ἱστορεῖ, λέγων· Γενομένων δὲ ἡμῶν ἐν Ἱερουσαλὴμ, ἀσμένως ἡμᾶς οἱ ἀδελφοὶ ἐδέξαντο. Τῇ τε ἐπιούσῃ εἰσῇειν ὁ Παῦλος σὺν ἡμῖν πρὸς Ἰάκωβον, πάντες τε παραγεγόνεισαν οἱ πρεσβύτεροι, καὶ ἀσπασάμενος αὐτοὺς, ἐξηγεῖτο καθ᾽ ἓν ἕκαστον ὧν ἐποίησεν ὁ Θεὸς ἐν τοῖς ἔθνεσι διὰ τῆς διακονίας αὐτοῦ. Τοῦτον δὴ οὖν τὸν ἀσπασμὸν, τιμητικὴν λέγει προσκύνησιν, ἣν ἀλλήλοις προσφέρομεν· ἐφ᾽ ἧς φησι περὶ Ἰακὼβ, ὅτι προσεκύνησεν ἐπὶ τὸ ἄκρον τῆς ῥάβδου αὐτοῦ. Οἷς ἀκόλουθα φθέγγεται καὶ Γρηγόριος, ὁ ἐπίκλην Θεολόγος, λέγων· Προσκύνησον τὴν Βηθλεὲμ, προσκύνησον τὸ σπήλαιον.
Now who of those rightly and sincerely understanding the Divine Scriptures, has ever supposed that these examples which we have cited speak of the worship in spirit? [Certainly no one has ever thought so] except perhaps some persons utterly bereft of sense and ignorant of all knowledge of the Scriptures and of the teaching of the Fathers. Surely Jacob did not adore the top of his staff; and surely Gregory Theologus does not bid us to adore the manger? By no means.
¶188
Τίς οὖν τῶν ὀρθῶς καὶ ἀπλανῶς τὰς θείας Γραφὰς νοούντων, καὶ τούτοις ἠκολουθηκὼς οἷς μαρτυρίοις ἐχρησάμεθα, εἶπεν ἐπὶ ταύταις λατρείαν γεγονέναι; Μηδεὶς τῶν νοῦν ἐχόντων· ἀλλ᾽ εἴ τινες λίαν ἀναίσθητοι, καὶ πάσης Γραφῆς, καὶ πατρικῆς διδασκαλίας ὄντες ἀλλότριοι. Μήτι γὰρ Ἰακὼβ ἐλάτρευσε τῇ ῥάβδῳ; Μήτι Γρηγόριος ὁ Θεολόγος τῷ σπηλαίῳ λατρεύειν παρακελεύεται; Οὐδαμῶς.
Again, when offering salutations to the life-giving Cross, we together sing: We reverence your cross, O Lord, and we also reverence the spear which opened the life-giving side of your goodness. This is clearly but a salutation, and is so called, and its character is evinced by our touching the things mentioned with our lips. We grant that the word προσκύνησις is frequently found in the Divine Scriptures and in the writings of our learned and holy Fathers for the worship in spirit, since, being a word of many significations, it may be used to express that kind of reverence which is service. As there is also the veneration of honour, love and fear. In this sense it is, that we venerate your glorious and most noble majesty. So also there is another veneration which comes of fear alone, thus Jacob venerated Esau. Then there is the veneration of gratitude, as Abraham reverenced the sons of Heth, for the field which he received from them for a burying place for Sarah his wife. And finally, those looking to obtain some gift, venerate those who are above them, as Jacob venerated Pharaoh. Therefore because this term has these many significations, the Divine Scriptures teaching us, You shall venerate the Lord your God, and him only shall you serve, says simply that veneration is to be given to God, but does not add the word only; for veneration being a word of wide meaning is an ambiguous term; but it goes on to say you shall serve him only, for to God alone do we render latria.
¶189
Πάλιν, τῷ ζωοποιῷ Σταυρῷ ἀσπασμὸν προσφέροντες, συνεπῳδάζομεν· Τὸν Σταυρόν σου προσκυνοῦμεν, Δέσποτα· καὶ προσκυνοῦμεν καὶ τὴν λόγχην, τὴν ἀνοίξασαν τῷ μύστῃ τὴν ζωοποιὸν πλευράν. Ὁ ἀσπασμὸς δηλονότι, καὶ αὕτη προσκύνησις καλεῖται, δείκνυταί τε διὰ χειλέων αὐτῶν ἁπτομένων. Οὐχ ὅτι δὲ μὴ εὕρηται πολλάκις ἐν τῇ θείᾳ Γραφῇ, καὶ τοῖς τῶν σοφῶν καὶ ἁγίων Πατέρων συγγράμμασι, καὶ ἐπὶ τῆς ἐν πνεύματι λατρείας· ἀλλ᾽, ἐπείπερ ἡ προσκύνησις πολλὰς διαφορὰς ἔχει, δύναται καὶ ἐπὶ ταύτης τάττεσθαι. Καὶ γάρ ἐστι καὶ ἡ κατὰ ἀξίαν τιμητικὴ προσκύνησις, ἀγάπης καὶ φόβου· ᾗ τῇ ὑμετέρᾳ ἐνδόξῳ καὶ κρατίστῃ μεγαλειότητι προσκυνοῦμεν. Ἔστι δὲ πάλιν φοβητική, καθ᾽ ἣν Ἰακὼβ προσεκύνησε τῷ Ἠσαῦ. Καὶ εὐχαριστική, καθ᾽ ἣν Ἀβραὰμ προσεκύνησε τοῖς υἱοῖς Χέτ, περὶ τοῦ σπηλαίου, οὗ παρέσχον αὐτῷ εἰς ταφὴν Σάρρας τῆς γυναικὸς αὐτοῦ. Καὶ δωροληπτικὴ δή τίς ἐστι, καθ᾽ ἣν οἱ λαμβάνοντές τι προσκυνοῦσι τοὺς ὑπερέχοντας, ὡς Ἰακὼβ προσεκύνησε τῷ Φαραώ. Πλειόνων οὖν οὕτω διαφορῶν οὐσῶν τῆς προσκυνήσεως, ἡ θεία Γραφὴ, Κύριον τὸν Θεόν σου προσκυνήσεις, καὶ αὐτῷ μόνῳ λατρεύσεις· μόνην προσκύνησιν τῷ Θεῷ προσενεχθῆναι νενομοθέτηκε, μὴ προστιθεῖσα τῷ προσκυνήσεις τὸ μόνῳ· πολλαχῶς γὰρ ὂν, ἀμφίβολόν ἐστι. Προστίθησι δὲ, καὶ αὐτῷ μόνῳ λατρεύσεις· λατρεία γὰρ μόνῳ τῷ Θεῷ παρὰ πάντων ἀναπέμπεται.
The things which we have decreed, being thus well supported, it is confessedly and beyond all question acceptable and well-pleasing before God, that the images of our Lord Jesus Christ as man, and those of the undefiled Mother of God, the ever-virgin Mary, and of the honourable Angels and of all Saints, should be venerated and saluted. And if anyone does not so believe, but undertakes to debate the matter further and is evil affected with regard to the veneration due the sacred images, such an one our holy ecumenical council (fortified by the inward working of the Spirit of God, and by the traditions of the Fathers and of the Church) anathematises. Now anathema is nothing less than complete separation from God. For if any are quarrelsome and will not obediently accept what has now been decreed, they but kick against the pricks, and injure their own souls in their fighting against Christ. And in taking pleasure at the insults which are offered to the Church, they clearly show themselves to be of those who madly make war upon piety, and are therefore to be regarded as in the same category with the heretics of old times, and their companions and brethren in ungodliness.
¶190
Τούτων οὕτως ἐχόντων, ἅπερ ὡρίσαμεν, συγχωρήσει Θεοῦ, ἐναργῶς ἀναπέφηνεν εὐπρόσδεκτα καὶ εὐάρεστα τῷ Θεῷ, προσκυνεῖσθαι καὶ ἀσπάζεσθαι τὰς εἰκόνας τοῦ Κυρίου ἡμῶν Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ, ὡς ἀνθρωπήσαντος, καὶ τῆς ἀχράντου Θεοτόκου, τῆς ἀειπαρθένου Μαρίας, τῶν τε τιμίων Ἀγγέλων, καὶ πάντων ἁγίων. Εἰ δέ τις μὴ οὕτω πιστεύει, ἀλλὰ φιλονεικότερον λοιπὸν διακείσεται, καὶ κακῶς φρονήσει περὶ τῆς προσκυνήσεως τῶν σεπτῶν εἰκόνων, τοῦτον ἡ ἁγία ἡμῶν οἰκουμενικὴ σύνοδος, τῇ ἐνεργούσῃ δυνάμει τοῦ Πνεύματος τοῦ Θεοῦ, ταῖς τε τῶν Πατέρων παραδόσεσι, καὶ τῇ τῆς ἐκκλησίας, ἀναθεματίζει. Ἀνάθεμα δέ ἐστι, χωρισμὸς ἀπὸ Θεοῦ παντελής. Εἰ γάρ τινες φιλόνεικοι ὄντες, μὴ πείθονται, μηδὲ παραδέχονται ταῦτα ἃ νῦν ὡρίσθησαν, κεντρίαν τινὰ πάσχοντες, τὰς ἑαυτῶν κατὰ Χριστοῦ ψυχὰς τραυματίας ποιοῦσι· καὶ ταῖς προσπιπτούσαις ταῖς ἐκκλησίαις ὕβρεσι χαίροντες, δηλονότι τῶν παλαιῶν αἱρετικῶν μανομένων εἰς εὐσέβειαν, συνάρχοντες ἐδείχθησαν, συγκαταριθμούμενοί τε τούτοις, καὶ σύμψυχοι τῆς δυσσεβείας γενησόμενοι.
We have sent our brethren and fellow priests, God-beloved Bishops, together with certain of the Hegumenoi and clergy, that they may give a full report of our proceedings to your godly-hearing ears. In proof and confirmation of what we have decreed, and also for the assurance of your most religious majesty, we have submitted proofs from the Fathers, a few of the many we have gathered together in illustration of the brightly shining truth.
¶191
Ἀπεστείλαμεν δὲ τοὺς ἀδελφοὺς ἡμῶν καὶ συλλειτουργοὺς, θεοφιλεῖς ἐπισκόπους, σὺν τισὶ τῶν ἡγουμένων καὶ κληρικῶν, ὅπως πληροφορήσωσι τὰ πάντα πρὸς τὰς θεοφιλεῖς ὑμῶν ἀκοάς. Εἰς πίστιν δὲ καὶ βεβαίωσιν ὧν ὡρίσαμεν, καὶ πληροφορίαν τῆς εὐαγοῦς ὑμῶν βασιλείας, μαρτυρίας πατρικὰς ἐξ ἁπάντων συναγαγόντες, μικρὸν μέρος τῆς φανεροτάτης ἀληθείας προσεθήκαμεν.
And now may the Saviour of us all, who reigns with you and who was pleased to vouchsafe his peace to the Churches through you, preserve your kingdom for many years, and also your council, princes, and faithful army, and the whole estate of the empire; and may he also give you victory over all your enemies. For he it is, who says: As I live, says the Lord, they that glorify me, I will glorify. He it is also who has girded you with strength, and will smite all your enemies, and make your people to rejoice.
¶192
Αὐτὸς οὖν ὁ πάντων Σωτὴρ, ὁ συμβασιλεύων ὑμῖν, καὶ εὐδοκήσας δωρήσασθαι ταῖς ἐκκλησίαις τὴν εἰρήνην δι᾽ ὑμῶν, πληθύναι ὑμῶν τὰ ἔτη, καὶ τοῦ συνεδρίου ὑμῶν, καὶ τῶν ἀρχόντων, καὶ τοῦ πιστοῦ στρατοῦ, καὶ πάσης τῆς πολιτείας, δῴη τε ὑμῖν κατὰ πάντων τῶν ἐχθρῶν τρόπαια. Ἔστι γὰρ αὐτὸς ὁ λέγων· Ζῶ ἐγώ, λέγει Κύριος, ἀλλ᾽ ἢ τοὺς δοξάζοντάς με δοξάσω. Αὐτός ἐστιν ὁ ζωννύων ὑμᾶς δύναμιν, καὶ πατάσσων πάντας τοὺς ἐχθροὺς ὑμῶν, καὶ εὐφραίνων τὸν λαὸν ὑμῶν.
And do, O city, the new Sion, rejoice and be glad; you that are the wonder of the whole world. For although David has not reigned in you, nevertheless your pious princes here preside over your affairs as David would have done. The Lord is in the midst of you; may his name be blessed forever and ever. Amen.
¶193
Σύ τε, ἡ πόλις, ἡ νέα Σιὼν, εὐφραίνου καὶ ἀγαλλιῶ, τὸ θαῦμα τῆς οἰκουμένης. Εἰ γὰρ Δαυὶδ ἐν σοὶ μὴ ἐβασίλευσεν, ἀλλ᾽ οἱ εὐσεβεῖς σου βασιλεῖς ἐν σοὶ τὰ κατὰ Δαυὶδ νῦν διοικοῦσι. Κύριος ἐν μέσῳ σου· εὐλογημένον τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας τῶν αἰώνων. Ἀμήν.
